## 14:40:06 UTC

**Analyzing SQL selector matching issues****Examining execution scope and type inference challenges**

I’m weighing how executions are tracked only within certain known SQL roots, but unknown external calls risk false positives. The core problem is poor receiver type detection—many calls like `s.db.ExecContext` are ambiguous because selector receivers aren’t fully resolved. Receiver provenance must be improved, ideally using explicit scopes or go/types analysis. Also, the SQL parser doesn’t fully handle multi-statement DDL, causing errors despite valid cases in the repo. I’ll need to think about how to parse and represent multi-statement executions accurately.

## 14:40:15 UTC

**Parsing SQL statement targets****Analyzing SQL parsing challenges**

This chunk explores parsing complexities like handling UPDATE SET columns, distinguishing task status, challenges with normalization and semicolon splitting inside strings, and potential bugs in CTE operation detection. I’m also noting some compilation errors and rule handling for DDL statements.

## 14:40:22 UTC

**Analyzing statement and variable handling****Summarizing architectural challenges with closures and methods**

I’m outlining key blockers in receiver resolution, closure handling, and multi-statement parsing to focus messaging efficiently while balancing discovery through tests. This will help prioritize fixes over listing every minor bug.

**Analyzing sequence repeat limitations****Evaluating repeat handling with unknown ranges**

I'm unpacking how unknown range placeholders generate repeats when loop bodies append the same element but delayed closures can mutate the sequence unpredictably. The system uses an opaque unknown repeat to stay safe despite potential “evil” changes later. I’m exploring how static repeats refine this when possible, but preserving exact repeats is tricky when aliases or mutations happen later, so the approach favors sound over-approximation by marking repeats as unknown unless carefully verified.
